I just know it went on for a while before I completely crashed.\n\nDoctor: About how many consecutive days did that period of unusually high energy and very little sleep last?\n\nPatient: I really can't say for sure. Like I said, I wasn't keeping track of the days. I mean... I was just going to work at the plant every day and doing my job. It never got to a point where I needed to be in a hospital or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy period, did your behavior cause major problems or seriously impair your ability to function at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it did. I actually had to ask to be pulled out of a shift-planning meeting at one point. Occupational health ended up getting involved, because... well, I just wasn't able to carry out the basic production decisions that my role depends on.\n\nDoctor: Did that high-energy period last at least seven consecutive days?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know. Like I mentioned a minute ago, I just wasn't keeping track of the time. It might have been a full week, or maybe slightly less than that. I was only getting maybe... two or three hours a night? But I didn't feel tired at all. I would show up at the plant the next morning feeling completely sharp and charged. It honestly felt like sleep was just optional... or like it was just an inefficient use of my time when I had so much to get done.\n\nDoctor: How long did that period of unusually high energy and very little sleep last?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure, to be honest. It's... it's a bit hard to pin down an exact timeframe because I was just moving so fast and doing so much. I didn't really keep track of the days. I just know it went on for a while before I completely crashed.\n\nDoctor: About how many consecutive days did that period of unusually high energy and very little sleep last?\n\nPatient: I really can't say for sure. Like I said, I wasn't keeping track of the days. It felt like a long time, but I don't have an exact number for you.\n\nDoctor: Did you need to be hospitalized because of what was happening during that high-energy period?\n\nPatient: No, I wasn't. I mean... I was just going to work at the plant every day and doing my job. It never got to a point where I needed to be in a hospital or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy period, did your behavior cause major problems or seriously impair your ability to function at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it did. I actually had to ask to be pulled out of a shift-planning meeting at one point. Occupational health ended up getting involved, because... well, I just wasn't able to carry out the basic production decisions that my role depends on.\n\nDoctor: Did that high-energy period last at least seven consecutive days?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know. Like I mentioned a minute ago, I just wasn't keeping track of the time. It might have been a full week, or maybe slightly less than that. I just remember the whole thing being a blur at the plant, so I can't give you a definite yes or no on if it was seven days straight.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy period, did you believe things that others said were not true, or hear or see things that others could not?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I mean... I know I was overwhelmed and couldn't do my job, but I never saw or heard things that weren't there. And I didn't have any strange beliefs or anything. It was really just the intense energy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed or lost interest in most things nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes. Honestly, that's what I've been dealing with recently. It's been going on for a while now, definitely more than two weeks. Lately, I wake up in my temporary apartment with this... this heavy, hollow feeling. It's just there right when I open my eyes, like the day is already ruined before I even get in the car to drive to the plant. I just don't care about much of anything right now.\n\nDoctor: How much are your current depressive symptoms interfering with your ability to work and take care of your usual daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: It's... it's interfering a lot. I actually had to ask to be pulled out of a shift-planning meeting recently. I just couldn't focus. It got to the point where occupational health got involved, because... well, I just wasn't able to make the basic production decisions that my role depends on. I just couldn't do it.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back to that high-energy period, was there any stretch that clearly lasted seven consecutive days or longer?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure on the exact number of days, to be honest. I mean... I wasn't exactly keeping track of the calendar while it was happening. It was just this intense stretch of time, but I couldn't tell you for certain if it lasted a full seven days in a row.\n\nDoctor: Was there a period of at least four consecutive days when you had unusually high energy and needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure about four days either. Like I mentioned, I just... I didn't keep a close eye on the calendar. I know it went on for a little bit, and I definitely wasn't sleeping much during that time, but I couldn't swear to you that it was exactly four days in a row. I just don't have a clear timeline of it.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to manage basic self-care, such as bathing, dressing, and eating, despite feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Honestly, no, not really. I mean, I try, but... it's just so hard to find the energy for even the simplest things. Taking a shower feels like this huge, exhausting task. Most days, I just stay in whatever clothes I slept in. And eating... I just don't have the appetite, or I can't bring myself to actually make anything. I haven't heard voices or seen anything that wasn't actually there. And I haven't had any strange beliefs or anything that people told me were untrue. I mean... I'm just really down and completely exhausted, but my mind isn't playing tricks on me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Did anyone close to you, such as a family member or coworker, observe that high-energy period and tell you it lasted at least one week?\n\nPatient: No, nobody ever said anything like that to me. I mean, my coworkers and family might have noticed I was getting a lot done or talking faster, but no one ever sat me down and said, \"Hey, you've been acting like this for a week.\" Like I said before, I really just don't have a good sense of how long it lasted, and no one else pointed out a specific timeline to me either.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
